# SmartGro — Decision and Risk Register

| | |
|---|---|
| Status | **Living document**; supersedes the snapshot in `../phases/PHASE_0_DISCOVERY_REPORT.md` §5 |
| Last updated | 2026-11-02 |

**Who decides**
* **Owner:** the project owner, who approves phases, architecture, process and scope.
* **Client:** SmartGro, which supplies business facts, policies, assets and accounts.

**Classification**
* **Resolved:** decided, with evidence.
* **Requires owner approval:** needed to close Phase 1 or to start the next approved step.
* **Requires client input:** needed to close Phase 1 or to start Phase 2/3.
* **Deferred to Phase N:** not needed until that phase gate; it will be raised then.

A recommended default is **never** treated as an approved business fact. It becomes one only when the decider confirms it here.

---

### 0.5 Options to unblock Phase 1 (P-1, P-2 and P-3 adopted 2026-10-24; see §0.6)

| Option | Effect | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| P-1 | **Defer floors and real inventory to data entry.** The owner already said these "can be entered or completed later" (OD-08). AC-1.4 (ERD validated against real inventory) would move to the **Phase 5 entry gate**: the first property's real data is run through SG-INV-1.3 before any inventory code is merged. | **Recommended.** It removes the main Phase 1 blocker without guessing any floors. |
| P-2 | **Pricing:** either resend section 9, or approve that the project team drafts a pricing-configuration proposal (units, display, GST treatment, deposits, cancellation per category) **as proposed defaults for the owner to edit and approve** | resend if available; otherwise P-2 |
| P-3 | **Approve the pending items as recommended in one reply** ("approve all as recommended"), or list exceptions | saves a round-trip; see `../phases/PHASE_1_PROGRESS_REPORT.md` §5 |

### 0.17 Owner decisions — 2026-10-25 (round 13): "Record Q-OW-18 owner decisions"

Source: the owner's seven answers to Q-OW-18. **These decisions approve the intended policy. They do not authorise any change to live permissions or role assignments, and none of them is implemented.** How each is applied, the rules every capability follows, and the implementation and test gaps are in `../security/SECURITY_MODEL.md` §11.

In every decision, a role is authorised only once it has been **explicitly granted** the named capability. A role name alone authorises nothing.

| ID | Decision (owner's words) | Recorded as |
|---|---|---|
| OD-51 | "Enquiry owner assignment: Super Admin and Sales/CRM Manager." | `leads.assign` for the Super Admin and the Sales/CRM Executive role. Interpretation: OD-33 has no "Sales/CRM Manager" role, so the Sales/CRM Executive role is meant (Q-OW-19.1). |
| OD-52 | "Booking request approval: Super Admin and authorised Operations/Property Manager." | `bookings.approve` for the Super Admin, the Operations Manager and the Property Manager (assigned properties only). Sales/CRM Executives, Marketing and Finance cannot approve. Interpretations: rejection uses the same capability (Q-OW-19.3); "authorised" means the role is granted the capability (Q-OW-19.2). Approval always re-checks availability under lock; no role, including the Super Admin, can force a booking (BR-BK-5). |
| OD-53 | "Public contact details editing: Super Admin and Marketing Manager." | `settings.contact.manage` for the Super Admin and the Marketing Manager. |
| OD-54 | "Legal/GST editing: Super Admin and Finance Manager." | `settings.legal.manage` for the Super Admin and the Finance role (interpretation: OD-33's "Finance"; Q-OW-19.1). |
| OD-55 | "Legal/GST verification: A second authorised person distinct from the editor." | `settings.legal.verify` for the Super Admin and Finance. Interpretation: these are the roles OD-54 authorises for legal and GST details. The verifier must be a different person from everyone who edited the version, the Super Admin included. Derived rule: a later edit clears the verification. |
| OD-56 | "Publishing approved content: Marketing Manager may publish only after separate approval by another authorised person." | The Marketing Manager publishes (including scheduling and rollback) only a version that another person holding `content.approve` approved. ### 0.19 Owner decisions — 2026-10-25 (round 15): "Resolve Q-OW-20 and D-17"

Source: the owner's decisions OD-68 to OD-73, with IDs given by the owner, answering Q-OW-20 and D-17. **These are owner-approved policy decisions for documentation. Individual permission grants may be supported as optional exceptions, subject to the role's permitted scope. Role membership alone must not grant permissions that have not been explicitly assigned to that role." | Q-OW-20.1 | The §3.1 matrix is each role's **permitted scope**. Permissions assigned to a role apply to all its members. Individual exceptions are optional and limited to that scope. SECURITY_MODEL §11.2 rule 1 sets out:<br>• **precedence:** scope is the limit; exceptions only add; separation of duties applies on top;<br>• **revocation:** from the next request; an exception stops when the role that permits it is removed;<br>• **audit:** who, when, user, permission, scope and reason.<br>No implementation design was added. **Why:** it supersedes the round-14 per-user reading of OD-60, and the existing role-based code already matches it. |
| OD-69 | "Contact-detail changes require independent approval before becoming public. Super Admin and Marketing Manager may approve changes made by another person, subject to explicit permission grants. No person may approve their own change." | Q-OW-20.2 | Confirms the OD-64 interpretation. `settings.contact.approve` is "not own" for both roles. Publisher ≠ approver (OD-65). |
| OD-70 | "Public-facing brand-setting changes require independent approval before publication. Super Admin may approve brand-setting changes but may not publish a version they approved. The author may publish only if they are not the approver." | Q-OW-20.3 | The Super Admin's brand cell becomes M/A (not own). `brand.approve` is "not own" and `brand.publish` is "after another's approval" for the Super Admin and the Marketing Manager. **Why:** with one Marketing Manager, brand changes could otherwise never be approved. |
| OD-71 | "Changes to tracking IDs, analytics identifiers and advertising pixels require independent approval before they become active on the public website. Apply the same author/approver/publisher separation principles used for other public-facing settings." | Q-OW-20.4 | Tracking is no longer exempt; new `settings.tracking.*` rows. **Interpretation:** the approvers are the Super Admin and the Marketing Manager, "not own", as for contact details and brand settings. Approving an ID never adds a script domain to the CSP allow-list. Tags inside an external tag manager are outside this approval (Q-OW-21.2). |
| OD-72 | "A user who creates or submits a booking request or reschedule must not approve that same request or reschedule. Approval must be performed by another authorised person with explicit approval permission. Every booking or reschedule must re-check availability under the required inventory lock. No Super Admin override or forced approval may bypass availability or separation-of-duties rules." | Q-OW-20.5 | `bookings.approve` is "not own". A customer's request is approved by any authorised staff user. A request or reschedule entered by staff needs a different approver. An outside-hours override never skips approval or the re-check. **Interpretation:** rejection follows the same rule (OD-61), and the creator may still withdraw their own request. **Why:** it reverses the round-14 default that one person could do both. |
| OD-73 | "Resolve D-17 in favour of the existing separation-of-duties policy: the author and approver must be different people, and the approver must not publish the version they approved. The author may publish an approved version if they are not the approver. A two-person workflow is therefore permitted." | **D-17** | One approval by a non-author is enough; no second approval. SECURITY_MODEL §11.6 lists the permitted combinations (author A, approver B, publisher A or C) and the failure cases: self-approval; approver publishes; co-author approves; unapproved or changed version; scheduling or rollback by the approver; missing permission; any Super Admin override. |

### 0.24 Owner instruction — 2026-10-25 (round 23): read-only pre-push verification (recorded in round 24)

| ID | Finding |
|---|---|
| D-31 | **Destination conflict.** GitHub has no organisation named `Smartgro` (public API: 404). The name `smartgro` (GitHub names ignore capitalisation) is held by a **personal user account created 2017-11-03**. Names are unique across users and organisations, so an organisation called `Smartgro` cannot be created while that account holds it. Pushing to `Smartgro/smartgro` would put the code under a personal account, which conflicts with OD-29, OD-76 and OD-78. **Owner to resolve (R-21).** OD-78 is unchanged: it is the owner's decision. |

### 0.31 Owner decisions — 2026-10-26 (round 32): unpublishing and tracking

|---|---|
| OD-98 | **Approved: immediate unpublishing.** Owner's policy:<br>1. users authorised to publish a content type may unpublish published content of that type;<br>2. unpublishing requires a non-empty reason;<br>3. it removes the item from the public website immediately;<br>4. it is audited with the actor, timestamp, item, reason and previous publication state;<br>5. the content and its audit history are kept;<br>6. republishing follows the existing approval and publication workflow;<br>7. the action is protected by server-side authorisation and state checks;<br>8. a published testimonial must be removable immediately if consent is withdrawn.<br>**Scope:** at minimum testimonials and customer stories. **Interpretation:** no second person is needed to unpublish (removing content cannot publish anything unapproved); the approver-is-not-publisher rule (OD-65) applies to putting content live, not to taking it down. **Implementation:** not started; the smallest safe implementation is proposed to the owner in the round-32 report and awaits a go-ahead. Pages, articles and FAQs could use the same control but are **not** in the approved scope; menus, settings and the redirect list are changed by publishing a new version and do not need it. Supersedes the "Open" entry for OD-98 in §0.30. |
| OD-100 | **Approved: tracking stays disabled.** Tracking IDs may be configured and approved in the CMS. Analytics and advertising scripts stay disabled until the cookie-consent approach is agreed with legal counsel. **Approving a tracking ID is not permission to run its script.** No consent package, tracking integration or new infrastructure is added without the owner's explicit approval. **Still open (owner and counsel):** the consent approach. **Acceptance criteria before any tracking is enabled:** see `../security/SECURITY_MODEL.md` §11.7. Supersedes the "Open" entry for OD-100 in §0.30. |


### 0.32 Owner decisions — 2026-10-26 (round 33): unpublishing built with conditions

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| OD-98 | **Implement immediate unpublishing, with conditions, for testimonials and customer stories only.** Use `testimonials.publish`; refuse deactivated accounts; require a non-empty reason; check that the item is live; change state under database locks. Unpublishing removes the item from all public output at once; keeps content and audit history; records the actor, time, reason, item and the version that was live; cancels scheduled publication; stops approved-but-unpublished versions from being published by returning them to the rejected state; and later publication needs the normal approval workflow. Republishing after consent is withdrawn must wait until consent is re-established and documented; if the current model cannot tell withdrawn from valid consent, the gap is documented and the smallest safe change proposed first (→ OD-101). No take-down control for other content types. **Status:** built locally on 2026-10-26 (`UnpublishContent`, "Take off the website" in the testimonial and customer-story editors), **not committed** (needs separate authorisation). |
| OD-101 | **Open (owner): recording withdrawn consent.** **→ Approved by the owner in §0.33 (round 34).** **Gap:** consent is stored per version (`consent_on_file`, `consent_reference`, and `statement_verified` for testimonials) and copied to the live record on publication. Nothing records that consent was withdrawn: after a take-down, the live record and every earlier version still say consent is on file with the old reference, so a new draft, a restored version or a revised rejected version starts with the old consent. The system cannot tell withdrawn consent from valid consent; only the two-person review stands in the way of republishing. **Proposed smallest safe change:**<br>1. additive, nullable columns `consent_withdrawn_at` and `consent_withdrawn_by` on `testimonials` and `case_studies` (no data change);<br>2. the take-down dialog asks "Was consent withdrawn?" and, if so, sets them (audited);<br>3. while they are set, every new draft of that item starts with the consent fields cleared, and a version can be submitted only with consent ticked, a consent reference different from the withdrawn one, and a confirmation that consent was given again after the withdrawal;<br>4. publishing such a version clears the withdrawal, audited with the new reference.<br>No new permission. **Simpler alternative (no migration):** treat every take-down of these two types as withdrawn consent, so later drafts always start with consent cleared and need a new reference; stricter, but it also forces new consent after a take-down for another reason, such as a typo. **Interim:** the editor of a taken-down item warns not to put it back until consent has been given again and recorded. Needed before the first real testimonial or customer story is published. |


### 0.33 Owner decisions — 2026-10-26 (round 34): OD-98 committed; withdrawn consent

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| OD-98 | **One local commit authorised and made** for the take-down feature, its tests and its documentation (`84a8f35`). |
| OD-101 | **Approved: record withdrawn consent with `consent_withdrawn_at` and `consent_withdrawn_by`** on testimonials and customer stories. The take-down asks explicitly whether consent was withdrawn; if yes, the time and person are recorded; if no, the consent record is kept. Every scheduled publication is cancelled and every submitted or approved version is returned; each step is audited; content and history are kept. While consent is withdrawn: new drafts do not inherit consent, restored versions do not bring it back, submission needs fresh consent evidence and an explicit reconfirmation, publication checks consent again on its own, all on the server and under locks; the normal approval workflow is needed before the item returns. The withdrawal is never cleared by creating or restoring a draft; re-establishing consent is an explicit, audited step. **Built on 2026-10-26; one local commit authorised in round 35 (§0.34).** **Engineering choices (OD-94):**<br>• the record lives on the item (`testimonials`, `case_studies`), which every version shares, so no version can overwrite it;<br>• "fresh evidence" means consent ticked, a consent reference that was **not part of the withdrawn consent** (not the live record's reference, and not the reference of any version that existed when consent was withdrawn; compared ignoring letter case and surrounding spaces), and the confirmation `consent_reconfirmed`, stored in the version only. A reference first used after the withdrawal stays usable if that version is rejected for another reason, so staff never need to invent a reference. A version created in the same second as the withdrawal counts as earlier, which can only refuse a reference;<br>• a draft that is in progress when consent is withdrawn is sent back as rejected, **unchanged**, like submitted and approved versions; drafts started later start without consent. No version's content is ever changed;<br>• **concurrency:** the withdrawn references are fixed when the take-down commits, because it locks the item and closes every version that exists then; an item has at most one open version; submission locks the version, publication locks the item, and the take-down locks both, so no concurrent request can reuse a withdrawn reference or slip between a check and its change (tested with parallel processes for approval, submission, publication and restore). References are compared within one item only;<br>• **a reference is only a pointer to evidence kept elsewhere; it is not proof that consent is valid.**<br>• consent is re-established when a version that passed these rules is published: its author confirmed it, another person approved it, and the publisher is recorded; the event `consent_reestablished` is audited and the record is cleared.<br>**Policy questions for the owner and counsel (not decided by the application):** what evidence counts as valid consent and how it is kept; whether the same person or company must give it again, and in what form; how long withdrawn consent records and the withdrawn content are kept, and whether withdrawn content must later be anonymised or deleted (the application keeps it, as OD-98 requires); who may withdraw consent for a customer story (the company, or a named contact); and whether withdrawal must also be recordable for an item that is not live (today it can only be recorded while taking a live item down). |

OD-36's optional per-user restriction is kept; only the default is reversed, following the directive ("do not grant broad access merely because an employee has a Sales role"). **Owner to confirm the default.** **Confirmed by OD-111 (b), 2026-10-27, superseding OD-36's default.** |
| OD-109 (d) | **Interpretation:** the matrix's "Totals" (Operations, Marketing) and "Deal values" (Finance) for `leads.view` are reports that do not exist yet, so those roles see no enquiries for now. Exports are Super Admin only (FR-CRM-6). |
| OD-109 (e) | **Interpretation (fail-closed, legal dependency D-15):** the enquiry form collects personal data, so it opens only once a privacy notice is published at `/legal/privacy`; the agreement and the notice version are stored with each enquiry. Only service contact about the enquiry is agreed; no marketing consent is asked (OD-100). No CAPTCHA service (Turnstile needs approval): CSRF, rate limits, a trap field and a minimum fill time instead. **No email notification is sent:** mail is configured as `log`, which would copy personal data into the log files; new enquiries show as a count in the back office. **Owner decisions needed:** privacy notice text (counsel), notification channel and mail service, retention period (BR-PRV-4, D-29). **Confirmed by OD-111 (c) and (d), 2026-10-27.** |
| OD-109 (f) | **Interpretation:** capacity, availability and prices are never shown or stored in profiles (FR-CMS-6, BR-SOT-1/3); pages say they are confirmed on request. The master plan's Phase 5 acceptance criteria that need real inventory and pricing (FR-INV-1…6, BR-AV/BR-PR unit tests, a real inventory import, 60-second propagation) stay **BLOCKED** by the unmet entry gate (OD-30, OD-32, OD-35, D-36): no floors, spaces or prices were invented. |
| OD-109 (g) | **Interpretation:** a property's photos and its offerings' photos must be SmartGro or commissioned photography uploaded **for that property** (`depicts_property_id`, set at upload and fixed); a Property Manager uploads photos only for assigned properties and sees no others. Replacing or removing a photo is a profile change and needs approval. |
| OD-109 (h) | **Interpretation:** category addresses follow the IA (`/workspaces/private-cabins`, `/workspaces/conference-training-rooms`); the seven categories of OD-07 are kept (meeting rooms and conference rooms stay separate, grouped together on the home page). |

(7) A camera position (`SG_CAM_<n>`) looks along its node's −Z axis, as glTF cameras do; the supplier needs to know this (CLIENT_INPUTS_REQUIRED §6). (8) Uploads in progress may be up to 32 MB (`config/livewire.php`; Livewire's default is 12 MB), so a model that is too large gets the plain-language reason. |

### 0.46 Owner decisions — 2026-10-28: 3D model limits (A-11) and the local database (A-12)

OD-118 is unchanged except where stated here. Instruction title: "SmartGro — Owner Decisions A-11 and A-12".

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| OD-119 | **A-11, owner's words (2026-10-28):** "I approve the proposed maximum of 500,000 triangles per model and 15 MB maximum upload size. Treat both as hard limits. Enforce validation server-side and before expensive processing. Validate the actual model geometry, file structure, format and resource usage; do not rely only on file extensions or browser-side validation. Apply appropriate safeguards for textures, materials, dimensions and processing time. Provide actionable validation errors and keep the limits configurable. Test representative models and viewer performance on mobile and desktop. Do not claim that these limits guarantee performance without evidence. Use docs/requirements/CLIENT_INPUTS_REQUIRED.md, item C-6.5, as the authoritative supplier file-requirements specification." |
| OD-119 (a) | **Approved hard limits: 500,000 triangles per model and 15 MB per file.** This settles OD-118 (f)(1) and (2): the triangle figure is no longer a proposal. A file over either limit is refused, with no override. The limits are configuration, not code. |
| OD-119 (b) | **C-6.5 (`../requirements/CLIENT_INPUTS_REQUIRED.md`) is the authoritative supplier file specification.** The automated checks enforce what C-6.5 states; changing a limit means changing both. |
| OD-119 (c) | **Not a performance guarantee.** The limits bound what a file may contain; they do not show that a model meets NFR-PERF-4 ("viewer interactive ≤ 5 s on a mid-range phone over 4G"). Only measurements may be cited, with the conditions they were taken under. |
| OD-119 (d) | **Safeguard values chosen under OD-119 ("Apply appropriate safeguards for textures, materials, dimensions and processing time"), developer defaults that the owner may change** (`config/twin.php`, stated in C-6.5): at most 500 pieces drawn separately (one for each part of each mesh shown); 100 materials; 64 textures, each PNG, JPEG, WebP or KTX2 (recognised by its bytes) of at most 4,096 pixels on its longest side, and 33,554,432 texture pixels in all (eight textures of 2048 × 2048); 128 MB of geometry once decompressed; 10,000 nodes; a 4 MB description, measured before it is decoded; a longest side of 1 m to 1,000 m (glTF's unit is the metre, so a model exported in millimetres or kilometres is caught); 10 seconds for all the checks of one file. **Interpretations:** (1) "before expensive processing": the size is read from the file system before any byte, the description is measured before it is decoded, nothing is decompressed on the server, and nothing is stored or written to the database until every check passes. (2) "the actual model geometry, file structure, format": the file type comes from the GLB header and each texture's type from its own bytes, never from a name or declared type; every reference and byte range is checked; the triangles of a Draco mesh are read from its compressed stream, because the viewer draws the decoded mesh whatever the description declares. A Meshopt stream is not decoded, so a stream that does not match its description fails only in the viewer. (3) The 15 MB limit is inclusive: a file of exactly 15 MB (15,728,640 bytes) is accepted. |
| OD-119 (e) | **Built 2026-10-28 (local, uncommitted; record: `../phases/PHASE_STATUS.md`, Phase 7):** limits in configuration (`config/twin.php`, `TwinModelLimits`; the two approved limits also through `TWIN_MODEL_MAX_MEGABYTES` and `TWIN_MODEL_MAX_TRIANGLES`); the checks extended (`GlbValidator`, `ImageDimensions`, `DracoStream`) with a plain-language reason for each; the model page shows each figure against the limit it was held to; C-6.5 rewritten as the supplier specification, with a test that fails if it and the configuration disagree. **Defect found and fixed:** the media library refuses files over 10 MB by default, so a model between 10 and 15 MB passed every check and was then not stored ("The model could not be stored"). Found by the browser checks' model at the limits; fixed with `config/media-library.php` (32 MB, the upload limit in progress); a test now stores a model of exactly 15 MB, and the upload logs the cause if storing ever fails. The browser checks' runner now stops when seeding fails (the failure had ended with exit code 0). |
| OD-119 (f) | **Measurements with test-only models (2026-10-28; `../testing/evidence/phase7/2026-10-28-od119/performance.json`).** Conditions: headless Chromium 151 drawing with the SwiftShader software renderer on an Intel i7-8650U laptop (load average about 4.7, partly another project's tests); the local server does not compress; generated test models, not supplier files. Models: small (2 kB, 36 triangles; Meshopt and Draco), typical size (2.72 MB, 100,000 triangles, 50 pieces, four 1024-pixel textures) and at the limits (14.85 MB, 15,568,664 bytes, just under the 15 MB limit; 500,000 triangles, 250 pieces, eight 2048-pixel textures, 33,554,432 texture pixels). **Desktop** (1440 px, no throttling), from pressing "Show the 3D model" to "Model shown": 0.19 s, 0.32 s, 0.86 s; one frame while the view moves: 24 ms, 130 ms, 602 ms. **Phone** (360 px at pixel ratio 2, page CPU slowed 4×, Lighthouse's slow-4G throttling: 562.5 ms latency, 1,474.56 kbit/s down): 5.3 s, 20.9 s, 89.7 s; the viewer module alone took 4.4 s to download (725 kB uncompressed) and the 14.85 MB file 83 s. Uploading the at-the-limits file with every check took 58 ms. Each model was shown in one render, drawing exactly the triangles counted at upload. **What this shows:** at that network speed a model needs about 5.6 s per MB to download, so NFR-PERF-4's 5 s cannot be met by a 15 MB model, nor by the 2.72 MB one. **What it does not show:** anything about a phone's GPU (the software renderer is not one, and the CPU slowdown does not apply to it) or a faster 4G connection. No limit here is claimed to guarantee performance (OD-119 c). Owner action A-13. |
| OD-120 | **A-12, owner's words (2026-10-28):** "I do NOT authorise running the two new migrations or php artisan access:sync against smart_go. Keep the 3D screens hidden on smart_go. Continue development and verification using smartgro_testing and smartgro_preview, after positively verifying the active database configuration. Do not modify smart_go. Do not run migrations, seeders, resets or access-sync operations against it." |
| OD-120 (a) | **`smart_go` keeps no 3D tables and no 3D permissions,** so the "3D models" screen stays hidden there. Phase 7 development and verification use only `smartgro_testing` (tests and browser checks) and `smartgro_preview`, each after confirming which database is active. |

Bytes for a Meshopt model 728 → 667 kB, for a Draco model 1,072 → 899 kB. Every model shown in one render, drawing exactly the triangles counted at upload. The requirement is unchanged. On the emulated profile, only the two 2 kB models stay under 5 s, and only with compression; at 1.47 Mbit/s the transfer of a 15 MB file alone takes about 84 s. **A-13 stays open.** |

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| OD-122 | **Owner instruction (2026-10-28), pasted, titled "SmartGro — next execution: isolate Laravel Boost, resolve the A-13 decision requirements, and continue Phase 6/7 work".** (A) Inspect Boost's MCP and the cache configuration, find the connection and cache store the tooling uses, stop Boost writing to `smart_go` with an isolated tooling cache or another safe, supported configuration, verify it in an isolated environment, leave the application's configuration and the owner's running server undisturbed, and document the incident; no cache clearing, no deleting the cache row, no other write to `smart_go`; "Do not claim `smart_go` was completely untouched." (B) Keep 15 MB and 500,000 triangles; preserve and reproduce the performance evidence; look for remaining startup and download bottlenecks without dependencies; document NFR-PERF-4's exact ambiguity (start and end of the measurement, device, network) without changing it or claiming it passes; "Keep the public viewer gated until the required performance conditions are satisfied or an authorised decision changes them." (C) Finish the remaining code-completable Phase 6 work; keep blocked items precise; invent nothing. (D) Continue every Phase 7 requirement whose dependencies are met, on isolated databases with test-only assets, keeping authorisation, private file access and zero 3D bytes; no real space anchors or public scenes without the owner's inputs; no public viewer while its performance acceptance is open. (E) Tests after changes, the full suite at the end, and a report. No commit, push, deploy, GitHub access, reset or history rewrite. |
| OD-122 (a) | **Laravel Boost cache incident and remedy.** **How it happened:** Claude Code starts Boost's MCP server (Laravel Boost 2.10.3) from `.mcp.json` as `php artisan boost:mcp` with no environment of its own, so it reads the project's `.env`: connection `mysql`, database `smart_go`, `CACHE_STORE=database`, that is `smart_go`'s `cache` table. Three parts of the tooling write to that store: Laravel Roster 1.0 caches its project scan for one hour (`roster:project:v3:…`) when a Boost tool or prompt asks for it; Boost's `database-schema` tool caches for 20 seconds (`boost:mcp:database-schema:…`); its `last-error` tool stores `boost:last_error` without expiry. Ordinary `artisan` commands, the tests (`CACHE_STORE=array`) and the application itself never trigger the scan. **What reached `smart_go`** (read-only check, 2026-10-28 15:4x): rows in its `cache` table only: Roster scans written at 2026-10-27 19:45:11 and 2026-10-28 13:47:10 (when the Boost server started with a working session), and `database-schema` entries from that tool's use by these working sessions on 2026-10-27 (expiring 10:28:02 and 16:16:43). No application table, row, column, migration or permission changed. **`smart_go` was therefore not completely untouched:** its `cache` table was written by development tooling. The rows were neither deleted nor cleared (instruction A). **Remedy:** `.mcp.json` gives the Boost server its own environment: `CACHE_STORE=array` (a cache in memory, nothing stored) and `APP_CONFIG_CACHE` pointing at a file that is never created, so a cached configuration (`artisan optimize`) cannot bring the database store back. Process variables take precedence over `.env` in Laravel. Boost's `database-query` tool still reads `smart_go`, with read-only statements only. **Verified in isolation** (`smartgro_testing`, each run first confirming its database with `SELECT DATABASE()`): with the remedy, prompts, `application-info` and `database-schema` left the test database's `cache` table unchanged (1 row before and after); without it (control), the same calls added 2 rows (a Roster scan and a schema entry). `smart_go`'s last write stayed at 13:47:10 throughout. **Not changed:** `.env`, `config/cache.php`, the application's cache store, the owner's server (none of SmartGro's was running; port 8000 belongs to another project). **When it applies:** from the next start of the Boost server (a new working session, or reconnecting the MCP server). The Boost process of the current session started at 15:41, before the change; no Boost tool was used after it, and `smart_go` was checked through a read-only database session instead. **Still possible:** Boost's `tinker` tool runs any code against `smart_go` and its `record-rule` tool writes files; neither was used. They can be switched off with `boost.mcp.tools.exclude` (the owner's choice). |
| OD-122 (b) | **NFR-PERF-4: what it leaves undefined (A-13), recorded without changing it.** Text: "default GLB budget per floor ≤ 15 MB compressed, viewer interactive ≤ 5 s on a mid-range phone over 4G [DEFAULT; validated in Phase 7]". (1) **Start:** not stated: opening the page, pressing "Show the 3D model", or (public viewer) the viewer scrolling into view, which BR-3D-6 allows. Measured here: from the press (mark `twin:requested`). (2) **End, "interactive":** not stated: the first frame, the controls answering input, or all textures loaded. Measured here: "Model shown", after the first render with the controls attached (`twin:usable`); the first frame is recorded too. (3) **Device, "mid-range phone":** no model, CPU or GPU class, browser or screen. Measured here: headless Chromium on a laptop (Intel i7-8650U), the page's CPU slowed 4×, a software renderer the slowdown does not reach, 360 px at pixel ratio 2; not a phone. (4) **Network, "4G":** no speed, latency or loss. Measured here: Lighthouse's slow-4G throttling (562.5 ms latency, 1,474.56 kbit/s down, 675 kbit/s up); at that speed a file takes about 5.6 s per MB. (5) **Cache:** first visit or return visit not stated. Measured here: first visit (a fresh browser profile per case). (6) **Server:** not stated; compression matters (viewer code 667 kB uncompressed, 159 kB with gzip, 133 kB with Brotli). Measured here: the local server (no compression) and a gzip stand-in. (7) **Scope:** "per floor" while a public scene may show several floors; the public viewer is not built. Measured here: one model in the back-office preview. (8) **Sample:** no count or percentile. Measured here: one run per case, repeated once (OD-122 e). **What would make it testable:** the owner's choice of each item (for example: from the press to the first frame with the controls live; a named phone class; a stated 4G speed and latency; first visit; median of three runs), staging with production compression (P6-6), and the pilot scene (D-13). The requirement is unchanged; it is not claimed met. **Gate kept:** the public viewer is not built, and it stays off the website until NFR-PERF-4's conditions are defined and met or the owner decides otherwise (A-13). Public pages load no 3D (tests and browser checks). |

| OD-123 (d) | **Phase 7 audited (PHASE_STATUS Phase 7; ADR-0009).** Confirmed: the limits are 15 MB and 500,000 triangles in configuration, with boundary tests (exactly 15 MB stored, 4 bytes more refused; 500,000 triangles accepted, 500,001 refused) and a test that pins the defaults to the approved values; the overrides `TWIN_MODEL_MAX_MEGABYTES` and `TWIN_MODEL_MAX_TRIANGLES` exist (runbook, OD-119) and are set in no environment file; malformed files are refused with reasons (unit tests of every check); the model file reaches only signed-in staff with two-step login and the view permission, never cached, never through the storage address (tests; a deactivated account added); nothing 3D before the press (browser checks). **Defects fixed in the viewer** (`resources/js/twin-viewer.js`, `resources/views/filament/twin-models/viewer.blade.php`), both shown in the browser before and after: (1) "Stop loading" pressed after the download, while a decoder loaded or the model was parsed: the page said "Loading stopped", but the unfinished viewer then showed its canvas anyway; an aborted viewer now never touches the page. (2) Each press created a graphics context that was never given back: after 16 rounds of "Show" and "Stop loading", Chromium dropped the oldest ("Too many active WebGL contexts", 5 times in 20 rounds), and that context's handler stopped the round on screen with a false "the device ran out of graphics memory" while its download went on unseen; the viewer now gives its context back when it stops or fails, removes its handler first, and a reset stops any download still running. After the fix: 20 rounds, 0 warnings, 0 false messages, the next press shows the model. **Checked for the first time:** a decoder module or its WebAssembly file that cannot load, and a browser without WebGL: each gives its plain-language message and leaves the page as before the press. All three are now part of `verify-admin.mjs`. No status changed: FR-3D-1 PASS on local evidence; FR-3D-2 and FR-3D-5 NOT BUILT; FR-3D-3 and FR-3D-4 PARTIAL; AC 2 NOT TESTED; AC 3 PASS on the pages checked; AC 4 BLOCKED (D-13, A-13). The public viewer stays unbuilt and off the website. |
| OD-123 (e) | **Verification (2026-10-28):** full suite on `smartgro_testing` (forced by `phpunit.xml`; configuration, route and event caches bypassed): **1,096 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (4,086 assertions, exit code 0)**, 513 s, 17:54 to 18:03, on the final code (15 more than OD-122: the tests of OD-123 b and d). Pint passed; `npm run build` exit 0; `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities; no dependency file changed; nothing installed. Browser checks (`../testing/evidence/phase7/2026-10-28-od123/``checks/`, headless Chromium 156.0.8078.47, the installed snap, `smartgro_testing`, 17:48 to 17:54, exit code 0, no failures): 28 public addresses at four widths, 112 loads, 0 axe violations in 56 runs plus 10 error-page runs, 0 overflow, 0 external and **0 3D requests**, 28 of 28 with their content without JavaScript, the topic page 200 at every width; the enquiry form and confirmation (3 runs); 32 back-office screen checks, 0 axe violations, nothing 3D before the press; the three viewers drawn; loading states; stopping while the decoder loads (nothing appears), 20 rounds of Show and Stop (all clean, 0 dropped contexts, the model shown after), a decoder module and its WebAssembly file that cannot load, no WebGL (plain messages); uploads through the form (at the limits stored, 4 bytes over 15 MB refused). Viewer defects before and after: `../testing/evidence/phase7/2026-10-28-od123/``teardown/`. **Databases written by this work:** `smartgro_testing` only (tests, browser checks, the isolated Boost runs and their controls). **`smart_go`:** read-only checks at 17:28 and 18:05: 44 migrations, batch 10, 43 tables, 34 permissions, no 3D table or permission; `cache` last written 2026-10-28 13:47:10 (the tooling row of OD-122 a), none since; `sessions` and `cache_locks` written by the owner's server on port 8001 (OD-123 a). It was not completely untouched over the project's life (OD-122 a); this round did not write to it. **`smartgro_preview`:** not touched by this work. It was written meanwhile by other activity: the documented preview server (port 8766, `LOCAL_DEVELOPMENT.md`) was started at 17:52:07 outside this session, 54 seconds after the other Claude Code session in this project opened; the preview recorded a role-capability synchronisation at 17:52:02 and a failed two-step login at 17:56:16. Left as found. **Git:** branch `main`, HEAD `8a45ae6`; 43 status entries, all uncommitted; nothing committed, pushed or deployed; no stash; remote unchanged. |

### 0.50 Owner instruction — 2026-10-28: two-step login optional for now

OD-120 and OD-123 are unchanged except where stated here.

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| OD-124 | **Owner's words (2026-10-28)**, after an emailed two-step code was refused on the preview: "remove this as of now, no verification needed, will apply it later, give an option in the setting only to go for MFA verification". Asked two questions, the owner chose: (1) the switch lives in the back office under Settings; a Super Admin turns it on or off, it starts off, and every change is audited (rather than an `.env` setting, or the profile page only); (2) production always requires two-step login, whatever the switch says. **This amends** "MFA required for every staff user" (SECURITY_MODEL §2, ADR-0003, SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE, API_CONTRACTS, AC-3.3), for local development only. |
| OD-124 (a) | **Built:** Settings → Security (`App\Filament\Clusters\Settings\Pages\Security`) shows whether two-step login is required, with "Require two-step login" and "Make two-step login optional" (each confirmed). Table `security_settings` (ERD C-32), one row. `ChangeTwoStepLoginRequirement`, authorised by `SecuritySettingPolicy` (`users.manage`: matrix row "Users, roles, MFA reset", Super Admin only; no new capability, so `access:sync` is not needed), audits `two_step_login_requirement_changed` in the `access` log, and records nothing when the value does not change. Filament still registers two-step login as required, so its set-up page and routes always exist; `EnsureTwoStepLoginWhenRequired` replaces Filament's check and asks `SecuritySetting::requiresTwoStepLogin()` on each request, so a cached route file never freezes the switch. Staff who set up two-step login keep it, are asked for it at every sign-in, and can remove it on their profile page. **Interpretation (derived):** "production" is read as every environment other than local development and the tests (`APP_ENV` other than `local` or `testing`), so staging also always requires it, and the page there offers no way to switch it off. A database without the table treats two-step login as optional and hides the page. |
| OD-124 (b) | **`smart_go`: not written** (OD-120). The owner's server on port 8001 runs without a route cache, so this code applies there at once: two-step login is optional, and Settings → Security is hidden (no `security_settings` table). Accounts there that already set up two-step login are still asked for it. |
| OD-124 (c) | **`smartgro_preview`, written by the Claude Code session that recorded OD-124 (the "other activity" in OD-123 (e)):** at 17:51, to start the documented preview on port 8766, the four migrations it lacked (`twin_models`, `twin_anchors`, `post_topics`, `posts.topic_ids`; this supersedes, for the preview, the "test database only" notes of ERD C-29 and C-31) and `access:sync` (17:52:02). At the owner's request, a new password for `admin@preview.test` (given to the owner in the session; not recorded here). One queued sign-in email sent by hand with `queue:work --once` (no queue worker runs for the preview). The failed two-step login at 17:56:16 is the owner's attempt with that code (see (d)). Then the `security_settings` migration and, through `ResetTwoFactorAuthentication`, two-step login cleared on the five preview staff accounts (five `two_factor_reset` entries, 18:14:39). Checked in headless Chromium: `admin@preview.test` signs in with the password alone and reaches the dashboard; Settings → Security shows "Two-step login is optional". |
| OD-124 (d) | **Found, not fixed (owner action A-14): emailed two-step codes are always refused outside the tests.** Filament stores each code's hash and expiry time (a date object) in the default cache store. `config/cache.php` has `'serializable_classes' => false` (the Laravel 13 default), so a store that serializes (`database` here, `redis` planned for production, ADR-0007) returns the date as `__PHP_Incomplete_Class`, and Filament refuses the code. Shown on `smartgro_preview` (a date written to and read back from its cache came back as `__PHP_Incomplete_Class`) and by the owner's refused code, entered 74 s after it was issued (valid for 4 minutes; no newer code was issued). `LoginTest` passes because the tests use the `array` store, which does not serialize. Authenticator-app codes were not checked. |
| OD-124 (e) | **Verification (2026-10-28):** 551 tests (`tests/Feature/Filament`, `Http`, `Providers`, `Actions/Access`, `Policies`, `Models`, `tests/Arch`) passed on `smartgro_testing` (caches bypassed), 18:11 to 18:14, after OD-123 (e)'s full run had ended at 18:03; the security tests passed again after each later change. Mutation check: without the middleware swap, "lets staff without two-step login in while two-step login is optional" fails. Pint passed. The full suite was not run. Nothing committed, pushed or deployed. |

The FR-CMS-5 and FR-CMS-6 rows of the pack were out of date (both built during Phase 5) and were corrected with their evidence. |

(2) **Enquiry form** (`EnquiryFormBlock`, `components/blocks/enquiry`): the Enquire page's form inside a page, sending to the same place under the same rules (`SubmitEnquiry`), optionally with a location and workspace type already chosen; it opens and closes with `/enquire` (a published privacy notice, D8.1, D-15): until then visitors see the same notice (now one component, `site/enquiry-closed-notice`) and the published contact details; an enquiry sent from it records the page as its landing page and keeps the page's campaign tags; wrong details bring the visitor back to the form (`#enquiry-form`) instead of the top of the page; in a staff preview the form is shown but cannot be sent (no enquiry from checking a draft); a page holds one, because the form's fields have fixed ids. Both are offered on ordinary pages only (not legal pages, articles or customer stories), like the contact details section. No new table, column, permission or package. |
| OD-125 (c) | **Defect fixed, introduced by OD-122: the insight topics broke the owner's local server.** The owner's server (port 8001) runs this working tree on `smart_go`, which has neither `post_topics` nor `posts.topic_ids` (OD-120; checked read-only). Unguarded, `/insights` queried `topic_ids` on every request, the article editor read the topic list, and publishing an article wrote `topic_ids`: each would fail there with a database error, and the Topics screen was offered and failed. Found from the code and `smart_go`'s schema, read-only; no request was sent to the owner's server. **Fix:** `PostTopic::isAvailable()` (the table and the column, asked once a request, the pattern of `SecuritySetting` in OD-124): without them the insights pages show no topics, topic addresses answer 404, the editor hides the topic field, versions neither read nor write `topic_ids`, and the Topics screen is refused. Test: with the table reported missing, the insights pages, the editor and the whole workflow to publication run without one SQL statement touching topics. Nothing changed on `smart_go`. |

A question is general (listed at /faqs, as before), about one location (shown on that location's page, before the general questions, IA §3.1 item 10), or about one workspace type (shown on that type's page); never both. Chosen in the FAQ editor under "Shown on", from lists of the locations on the website and the workspace types; set through the FAQ's versions like its other fields; a location or type with questions cannot be deleted. The property page lists "Questions about SmartGro {name}" (with an "On this page" link) and the category page "Questions about {type}"; nothing is shown where there is no question. Columns `faqs.property_id` and `faqs.product_id`, migrated on the test database only; where they are missing (the owner's `smart_go`, OD-120; the preview, until the owner has it migrated) every FAQ stays general and nothing reads or writes them (`Faq::hasScopes()`, tested). Structured data for FAQs (FAQPage) is Phase 11's (schema per template) and was not added. |

Browser checks (`../testing/evidence/phase4/2026-10-28-od125/checks/`, headless Chromium 156, the installed snap, `smartgro_testing`, 19:08 to 19:14, all exit code 0, no failures): 28 public addresses at four widths, 112 loads, 0 axe violations in 56 runs, no overflow, no external or 3D requests, 28 of 28 without JavaScript, the A-70 and hot desks pages with their fixture FAQs; the enquiry form and confirmation; **the page journey**: the editor offered both new sections, the Marketing Manager built and submitted the page, the Super Admin approved it, the author published it; at 360 and 1440 px the page showed both sections, three Noida location cards and the form with A-70 chosen, sending from that page with its campaign tag, 0 axe violations, no overflow, and the same without JavaScript; wrong details came back to `#enquiry-form` with the message by the field; the corrected enquiry reached the confirmation page with a reference; 32 back-office screen checks, 0 axe violations. An earlier run failed in the new check itself (it sent a fourth enquiry within a minute, which the route's limit of three refuses, and read the section list before opening it); the check was corrected, not the site. **Databases written by this work:** `smartgro_testing` only. **`smart_go`:** read-only at the end: 44 migrations, batch 10, 43 tables, 34 permissions; `cache` last written 13:47:10 (OD-122 a), `sessions` 17:50 (the owner's server); not written by this work. **`smartgro_preview`:** not touched by this work (last written 18:24:50, before this instruction, by OD-124's session); it lacks this round's FAQ columns, so FAQs stay general there until the owner has it migrated. **Git:** branch `main`, HEAD `8a45ae6`, 84 status entries (this round's, OD-124's and earlier rounds'), all uncommitted; nothing committed, pushed or deployed. |

### 0.53 Owner instruction — 2026-10-28: continue Phases 5 to 7 and onward without waiting for the Phase 4 decisions

OD-30, OD-32, OD-35, D-36, OD-112, OD-114, OD-118 to OD-126 unchanged.

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| OD-127 | **Owner instruction (2026-10-28), pasted:** "Continue SmartGro development immediately. Do not stop at Phase 4, and do not wait for A-15, A-16, A-17, or the moderated session to proceed with unrelated work already authorized under the original roadmap." It restates the Phases 0–15 roadmap and statuses; asks for the actionable Phase 5 work first (property, floor and workspace relationships, inventory, capacity, availability and pricing **structures**, concurrent inventory protection, "implement the configurable structures and mark missing business inputs explicitly"), then Phases 6 and 7, then previously authorised code-completable work in Phases 8–13; A-15 only if recorded as an authorised decision; A-16 without a new package or SVG upload; A-17 searched for and kept open; AC-4.2, A-14, OD-100 and OD-101 kept open; the standing safeguards (no writes, migrations, seeding, cache clearing or `access:sync` on `smart_go`; `smartgro_preview` inspected, not migrated without authorisation; no packages, commits, pushes or deploys; tracking off; the public 3D viewer gated; nothing invented); work in verified packages; and a requirements traceability matrix (started as `../testing/TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md`). |
| OD-127 (a) | **Decision checks before any work.** A-15: the register records only the options (OD-126 a); no owner choice is recorded, so **A-15 stays open and nothing was built for design tokens**; the palette (OD-42) is unchanged. A-16: neither PNG/WebP-only uploads nor an SVG-sanitising package has been approved, so **A-16 stays open**; the approved logo stays. A-17: searched the repository (every Markdown and text file) and, by file name, the owner's Documents, Downloads and Desktop folders (`addendum`, `smartgro`): nothing but this register and PHASE_STATUS mention it, so **A-17 stays open**; no reconciliation is claimed and no roadmap amendment is proposed. AC-4.2, A-14, OD-100 and OD-101 unchanged. |
| OD-127 (b) | **Interpretation: the Phase 5 entry gate.** PROJECT_MASTER_PLAN §2 gates Phase 5 on floors and the first property's real inventory supplied through SG-INV-1.3, pricing values PR-1 to PR-11 and the [DEFAULT] values confirmed (OD-30, OD-32, OD-35, D-36). OD-30 places the gate "before inventory code is merged". Read with this instruction ("implement the configurable structures and mark missing business inputs explicitly"): building and testing the inventory **structure** locally, with no data, is permitted; **merging it into the project's history waits for the gate**. The code is uncommitted. **If the owner commits the working tree, this inventory code would be merged before the gate; that needs the owner's own decision on OD-30.** No floor, space, seat, allocation, price or availability was entered on `smart_go` or `smartgro_preview`; tests and browser checks use labelled fixtures on `smartgro_testing` only. |
| OD-127 (c) | **Built (Phase 5, locally, not accepted).** Tables `space_types`, `floors`, `spaces` (soft-deleted, so codes are never reused), `space_seats`, `offering_space`, `space_allocations`, and `products.allowed_inventory_modes` (ERD C-34). Floors per building (FR-PROP-1, BR-PROP-6). Spaces with type, inventory mode (whole space, individual seats, shared pool, hybrid zone), capacity, area, status and "may be shown to customers" (FR-PROP-2, FR-PROP-3); the categories a space can be sold as, limited to its property's active offerings whose category suits the mode (BR-PRODCFG-2, BR-PRODCFG-3). Manual set-up on the property's page, including a numbered run of up to 100 spaces in one step (FR-INV-1, manual part). Status changes with a reason, audited and announced by a `SpaceAvailabilityChanged` event dispatched after commit (FR-INV-2, BR-SOT-4). Seats added or taken out of use with a reason. Allocations with an internal agreement reference, dates, notice, end-date changes and cancellation, never deleted (FR-INV-3). One availability service, `App\Services\Inventory\SpaceAvailability`, for staff counts and the customer states of BR-AV-1 (FR-INV-6), kept the only reader of allocations by an architecture test. Every write locks the space's row before reading (ADR-0004, BR-BK-5): BR-AV-2, BR-AV-3, BR-AV-3a, BR-AV-3c, BR-AV-3d and BR-AV-4 are checked for every day concerned; BR-INV-2 (permanent codes, never removing an allocated space), BR-INV-3 (no change of counting while allocated) and BR-INV-4 (supply reductions refused with the allocations concerned). Capabilities `inventory.manage`, `allocations.view` and `allocations.manage` from SECURITY_MODEL §3.1 (§11 rows added); back-office screens **Properties → Spaces**, **Space types**, and Floors and Spaces on each property's page; categories gain "Spaces it can be sold with". All hidden, and no inventory query run, on a database without these tables (the owner's server). |
| OD-127 (d) | **Interpretations.** (1) Floors follow §3.1 "Properties, buildings, floors" (`properties.manage`): a Property Manager edits their property's floors but does not add or remove them. (2) Space types are reference data like categories, so `products.manage` keeps them. (3) Marketing's part of §3.1 "Space visibility on website" (hide only, BR-SOT-3) waits until the website shows spaces (A-8). (4) BR-INV-1 "all changes are audited with a reason": a reason is required for every inventory change, additions included. (5) BR-INV-4's override with `bookings.override` is not built: every conflicting reduction is refused. (6) Customer states (BR-AV-1) are computed for staff only, not shown on the website (FR-DISC-3 waits for A-8, OD-112 d); BR-AV-2's 90-day horizon is used as an **unconfirmed [DEFAULT]** (D-36, `config/inventory.php`), and BR-AV-1 gives no threshold, so "Limited availability" is never shown. | OD-130 (a) | **Built (FR-SEO-3; locally, uncommitted, not accepted).** `GET /sitemap.xml` (`SearchEngineController`, `App\Services\Website\Sitemap`): a sitemaps.org 0.9 `urlset` built at each request from the same published records and queries as the pages (WorkspaceCatalogue; the published scopes), so a publication or a takedown shows at once: the home page; `/workspaces`, each category with a page and each category in each city where it is offered; `/locations`, each city with a property on the website, each sector with one, each property and each published offering; `/insights` with its further pages, each topic with a published article (with its further pages) and each published article; `/customers` and each published customer story with consent on file; `/faqs` when a general question is published; `/enquire`; `/book-a-tour` only while tour requests are open; every published CMS page whose version allows indexing. Each address is the canonical URL its page gives (built from `APP_URL`), listed once; `lastmod` only for articles, customer stories and CMS pages (their publication time); no `priority` or `changefreq`. `GET /robots.txt` replaces Laravel's static `public/robots.txt` (removed): in production every page may be crawled and the sitemap is named; elsewhere `Disallow: /`. `SetSecurityHeaders` adds `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` to every response outside production and to the back office in every environment, so the back office's address is never written in `robots.txt` (D-30). Lists of articles, customer stories or questions with nothing in them, and `/book-a-tour` while tours are not offered, now say `noindex` and are not listed; an article list page past the last one answers 404. No package installed: `spatie/laravel-sitemap` (OD-23, Phase 11) was not needed. |

So: (1) production allows every page to be crawled; private pages are kept out of search results by their own `noindex`, never by naming them in `robots.txt`; (2) every non-production environment forbids crawling and says `noindex` on every response (the runbook's staging rule applied to every environment that is not production); (3) **no rule for particular crawlers, such as AI or answer-engine crawlers**: none is decided, so all crawlers are treated alike (owner action A-21); (4) `/workspaces` and `/locations` are main sections of the website and stay indexable and listed even with nothing to show; the article, customer-story and question lists, which can stay empty after launch, say `noindex` while empty; (5) further pages of a list (`?page=2`) are listed, because each is an indexable page with its own canonical URL; (6) the sitemap is generated at each request rather than written to a file on publication (INTEGRATION_CONTRACTS lists sitemap regeneration on `ContentPublished`): the effect FR-SEO-3 asks for, "nothing unpublished", holds at every moment, and a CDN must not cache it for long (DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK §3). |

This is syntax and property validity only: it does not say a search engine will show a rich result. FAQ rich results in particular are shown by Google only for a few kinds of websites (government and health, since 2023), so SmartGro's FAQPage is not expected to produce one. (2) *Allow search and answer engines, refuse crawlers that only collect training data:* some companies publish separate names for each (OpenAI, for example, documents `OAI-SearchBot` for search and `GPTBot` for training; Google documents `Google-Extended` as the control for use in its AI models, separate from Google Search; Common Crawl uses `CCBot`); keeps answer-engine visibility; the list needs keeping up to date. (3) *Refuse AI crawlers altogether:* least use of the content, and less presence in AI answers. In every case `robots.txt` is a request that well-behaved crawlers follow, not access control, and it applies only in production (elsewhere everything is refused). Implementing the chosen option is a few lines in `SearchEngineController::robots()` and a test. |

tour requested, confirmed by staff, calendar file, moved, requested without JavaScript). *Route review:* all 106 back-office routes require sign-in; public writes (`/enquire`, `/book-a-tour`, a visitor's tour cancel and move) are rate limited, the visitor's also signed. *Code review:* no outbound HTTP call, tracking script or provider code in the application; raw SQL fragments use only column names fixed in code; no model is unguarded; scheduled commands do not overlap. **Findings:** **P14-1 (medium, open, needs approval):** `composer audit` reports `symfony/mime` CVE-2026-45070 (email header injection through parameter names; fixed in 7.4.22 and 8.1.10); SmartGro sends no email yet, so exposure today is low; updating it is a package change (owner approval). **P14-2 (known exception):** the PHPUnit advisory, development only (OD-112 c). **P14-3 (low, configuration):** the session cookie is marked Secure only when `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE=true`, and `APP_DEBUG` must be false in production: both now in the runbook's production checks. **P14-4 (low, local only):** Laravel Boost's `/_boost/browser-logs` accepts unauthenticated posts wherever the application runs locally with development packages (including the owner's server on port 8001); production installs without them. **P14-5 (verified, no defect):** the private disk (media originals, 3D models, inventory workbooks) is served at `/storage/{path}` only with a valid temporary signature: new `PrivateStorageTest` (unsigned and forged reads and an unsigned write refused). **P14-6 (test hygiene):** test files that commit their data leave 404 rows in the test database; the 404 monitor's tests clear the table first. Earlier defects found and fixed in this period: the simultaneous-new-visitor deadlock (OD-129 e), a follow-up due time stored in the wrong zone (OD-130 e). **Not done yet (Phase 14):** a penetration test or independent security review (AC-14.1), a manual WCAG 2.2 AA audit (AC-14.2; automated axe checks only), NFR measurements on staging (AC-14.3; staging does not exist, D-14), UAT with the owner (AC-14.4). |
| OD-133 | **Owner instruction (2026-10-29): "continue".** Interpreted as continuing the Phase 14 work OD-132 authorised; **not** as approving P14-1 (the `symfony/mime` update) or D-21, which stay open. |
| OD-133 (a) | **Migrations (Phase 14, data integrity).** On `smartgro_testing` only, after the target check confirmed that database with uncached configuration: `migrate:fresh` (68 migrations), then **the 22 migrations of this period (all uncommitted, the newest 22) rolled back and applied again cleanly** (22 down, 22 up; 68 applied, 0 pending). A full `migrate:refresh` ran every `down()` without error but could not re-create `activity_log`: **P14-7 (low, by design):** the published activity-log migration (Phase 3, committed) has no `down()`, so a full reset never drops the audit log; a full rollback is therefore not supported, and production rollbacks must go by steps (`migrate:rollback --step=N`, after a backup), never `migrate:reset`, `refresh` or `fresh` (DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK §4). The test database was restored with `migrate:fresh`. |
| OD-133 (b) | **Query growth (Phase 14, performance).** Measured: each address with a little content and with much more (3 locations, 7 workspaces, 9 articles, 14 questions, 5 customer stories, 32 enquiries). **P14-8 (medium, fixed):** the home page went from 18 to 36 queries, the workspace pages from 18 to 36 and 36 to 58, a property page from 27 to 37, the sitemap from 39 to 76 and the search titles report from 54 to 112. Causes: offerings listed through their property loaded that property again one by one; sectors their city; the workspace page its building; each profile its search fields; each profile without photos still ran a photo query; the sitemap loaded the property list twice. Fixed: `Property::publishedOfferings()` tells each offering its property (`chaperone`); the catalogue loads sectors' cities, buildings and profiles' search fields with the lists; `toRevisionPayload()` uses search fields already loaded (it reads them as before otherwise); no photo query without photos; the sitemap and the report load the property list once. New `QueryGrowthTest`: 18 addresses (12 public pages, the sitemap and 5 back-office lists and reports) run **the same number of queries** with little and with much more content. Not measured: a CMS page whose sections load media or locations still costs the report that page's own queries. |

| OD-137 | **Owner instruction (2026-10-30, pasted):** investigate the PHPUnit advisory (P14-2) and resolve it "if safely possible". Investigation and a proposed remediation only: no `composer update`, `require` or `install`; no edit of `composer.json` or `composer.lock`; no application code to work around it; explicit approval before any dependency change; documentation changes proposed, not made. Same protections as OD-136. **Then, the same day, answering the proposal:** the owner approved updating `pestphp/pest` to v5.3.4 and `phpunit/phpunit` to 13.4.5 as proposed, and all the proposed documentation changes. |
| OD-137 (a) | **Investigation (read-only).** `composer audit`: one advisory, PKSA-qhjm-rpqc-qkn6 / GHSA-9vw2-f4vp-x7gf / CVE-2026-119193 (CWE-379; GitHub severity medium, CVSS 4.0 base 5.4), PHPUnit 13.4.0 to 13.4.3, fixed in 13.4.4; installed and locked 13.4.3. When PHPT tests run with code coverage, PHPUnit runs each test's code from a fixed folder, `<temp>/phpunit-phpt`, even when another user created it, who can then replace the code. 13.4.4 also fixed the same weakness in the automatic coverage cache folder, likewise used only with code coverage. **Path:** development only; `pestphp/pest` (`^5.3`, also through `pestphp/pest-plugin-laravel`) and `brianium/paratest` (through Pest) require PHPUnit. **Production:** not affected (`composer install --no-dev`; no application code refers to PHPUnit; `public/` does not expose `vendor/`). **This machine:** `/tmp` is shared and service accounts run processes, but there are no PHPT tests and no coverage driver, and CI sets `coverage: none` on a single-user runner, so the vulnerable code was not reached (D-1's interim rule). **Upstream:** Pest v5.3.3 conflicts with PHPUnit above 13.4.3 (OD-112 c); Pest v5.3.4 (2026-10-27 11:04 UTC) requires `^13.4.5` and conflicts with anything above it, citing this advisory. D-1's condition was met. Only read commands were used (`composer audit`, `show`, `why`, `why-not`), with Packagist's and GitHub's public records. Details: `../testing/PHASE_14_ACCEPTANCE_PACK.md` §3. |
| OD-137 (b) | **Update carried out.** Dry run with scripts off: `composer update pestphp/pest:5.3.4 phpunit/phpunit:13.4.5 --dry-run --no-scripts`, exit code 0, two operations only. Then `composer update pestphp/pest:5.3.4 phpunit/phpunit:13.4.5`, exit code 0, with OD-136's environment overrides (database `smartgro_testing`, array cache, sessions and sync queue, every cache path in a scratch folder). Now Pest v5.3.4 (commit `159514bacefb`) and PHPUnit 13.4.5 (`941457a4f147`), locked and installed. The lock-file diff is in those two entries only: versions, commits, support links, times and their new requirements (Pest: `brianium/paratest` ^7.27.4, `phpunit/phpunit` ^13.4.5, `symfony/process` ^8.1.9, conflict `>13.4.5`; PHPUnit: `sebastian/git-state` ^1.1). The locked `brianium/paratest` 7.27.4, `symfony/process` v8.1.9 and `sebastian/git-state` 1.1.0 already met them, so nothing else changed; `content-hash` and `composer.json` unchanged. Checksums: of 1,244 working-tree files outside `vendor/`, `node_modules/`, `storage/` and `.git`, only `composer.lock` changed; the 1,457 compiled views identical; `bootstrap/cache` untouched. |

Full suite in the four foreground parts, target checked, each exit code 0, none interrupted: `tests/Feature/Actions` 354 (1,436 assertions); `tests/Feature/{Console,Database,Listeners,Models,Policies,Providers,Security,ExampleTest.php} tests/Unit tests/Arch` 240 (744); `tests/Feature/Infrastructure` 29 (97); `tests/Feature/{Filament,Http,Services,Views}` 644 (3,446): **1,267 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (5,723 assertions)**, the same counts as OD-136. | OD-139 (b) | **Built (local, uncommitted).** *Hero:* the tagline as the heading (OD-01), the offer in one sentence, "Find your workspace" (to the finder) and "Explore locations" (once a location is listed), facts from records (locations, workspace types, "Tours on request" when offered), a card listing the listed locations, and the image slot. *Workspace finder* (`GET /find-a-workspace`, `WorkspaceFinderController`, works without JavaScript): active workspace types and listed locations; leads to the offering's page, else the type's page, else the enquiry form with the choice filled in (when open), else the location's page; unknown or unlisted choices are ignored. *Locations:* one tile per listed location: its published main photo or a branded tile (its code and the SG mark, no building), the address, the number of published workspace types. *Workspaces:* the five families of `WorkspaceCatalogue::GROUPS` in an asymmetric grid, with each type's published locations ("Available at"), a published offering photo or a pictogram, and "Ask SmartGro" while the enquiry form is open. *Before you visit:* visits (tour form when offered), location pages, and "3D walkthroughs and floor plans: Not available yet" beside a floor-plan illustration captioned as one; the public viewer stays off (OD-118 d). *Get in touch* (`#contact`): enquiry and tour buttons only while those forms work, "An enquiry or a tour request is not a booking", the published contact details or "Contact details are on their way". *Every public page:* header restyled (pill buttons, larger logo from `lg`), footer on Deep Forest with the dark-background logo (OD-45) and contact details as their own column; menu contents unchanged. *Back end:* `WorkspaceCatalogue::mainPhotos()` (all main photos in one query, checked as on the property page), `TourForm::isOffered()` (one query; false without the tour tables), a "Tour request form" section for menus, `find-a-workspace` reserved for CMS pages, a `pill` option on `x-ui.button`. The anchors menus use (`#workspaces`, `#contact`, `#locations`, the five family anchors) are kept. |
| OD-139 (c) | **Interpretations for the owner to confirm or change.** (1) **Headings:** the reference uses an editorial serif. Playfair Display (SIL Open Font License, downloaded at build time and served from the site like Sora and Inter, so no third-party request) is used for the home page's headings only, through one token (`--font-editorial`); Sora and Inter (OD-43) stay everywhere else. Proposal P-OD139, DESIGN_SYSTEM §7.4. (2) **Images:** every image slot shows a published photo of a SmartGro location (real photography of that property, licence valid, BR-MED-2, BR-MED-3); until one exists, an illustration labelled "Illustration, not a photo of a SmartGro location", a branded tile or a pictogram (D-12). No stock, generated or old-website image was added. (3) **Finder:** workspace type and location only; team size and move-in date need verified inventory (OD-113 d). (4) **Menus stay CMS-published** (BR-CMS-9): the reference's labels (Spaces, Locations, Solutions, Community, About, Resources) were not hard-coded. A "Book a tour" header button can now be added under Website → Navigation (section "Tour request form", shown only while a tour can be requested). (5) **Left out:** site search (no search feature exists), "Watch our story" (no approved video), prices, live availability, occupancy figures, the floor selector and 3D hotspots (no published scene), other cities' cards (records list Noida only). |
| OD-139 (d) | **Verification (2026-10-30).** *Tests:* `HomeControllerTest` 11 (4 new: the finder, photography rules, form links only while the forms work, no tour tables as on `smart_go`), `WorkspaceFinderControllerTest` 6 (new), `SiteSectionsTest` +1, `PagePayloadTest` +1 case. Full suite in the four foreground parts, each exit code 0, none interrupted: 354 (1,436 assertions); 240 (744); 29 (97); 656 (3,526): **1,279 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (5,803 assertions)**. Pint passed; `npm run build` exit code 0. *Browser kit* (`../testing/evidence/phase6/2026-10-30-od139/`, each part on its own): public exit code 1 first (axe `definition-list`/`dlitem` on `/`: an icon inside a description-list group; fixed; kept as `checks-public-attempt1/`), then 0 (116 page loads, no failures; `/` 0 axe violations at 360 and 1440 px, no overflow at four widths, no external request, content without JavaScript); enquiry 0; tour 0; back office 0 (74 screens). 3D performance not run (viewer unchanged). *Screenshots and checks* (`../design/review/2026-10-30-od139/`): the fixture state at 1440, 1024, 768 and 390 px, the empty state of `smart_go` at 1440 and 390 px, the finder, the phone menu; no console error, failed or external request or overflow; 48 keyboard stops, each with a visible focus indicator; the finder reaches the expected pages with and without JavaScript. *Databases:* test writes on `smartgro_testing` only. `smart_go` not written by this work (read-only: unchanged schema; its new rows are the owner's OD-138 sign-in at 11:50 IST and guest sessions from a desktop browser at 11:51 and 12:22). `smartgro_preview` unchanged. No request to ports 8001 or 8003 or to the preview server. **The owner's server serves this working tree, so it shows the new home page since the build.** *Git:* HEAD `8a45ae6`; nothing committed, pushed or deployed; no package changed. |
| OD-139 (e) | **Still different from the reference, or not done:** no photography (the photo-led look needs SmartGro's photos: per location the entrance, reception, the coworking floor, a cabin, a meeting room and shared areas, landscape, at least 1,600 px wide, SmartGro or commissioned, with alt text and licence details, plus one wide hero image); inner pages keep their layouts and Sora headings (only the header and footer changed); menu labels are the published ones; no search, video, floor selector or prices; one city. Not done: a manual accessibility audit, staging measurements and the owner's review. **Implemented, not accepted.** |
